Use this whenever the user asks to review, cross-review, or get a second / independent opinion on a diff, pull request, branch, commit, or set of changed files — for correctness, edge cases, security, error handling, tests, or conventions — esp… ([powdream/claude-plugins](https://github.com/powdream/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/review-tools/skills/cross-code-review))
- **cross-code-runtime-benchmarking**: Use when validating a new forward-modelling tool against an established code: you need a single-realization runtime ratio plus fractional-agreement of model outputs (e.g. rotation/dispersion curves) on matched synthetic observations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/cross-code-runtime-benchmarking))
- **cross-code-simulation-comparison**: Use when two simulation codes' power spectra are available as |code_A/code_B − 1| versus k curves and you need to locate the wavenumber where they diverge beyond a threshold (e.g. 1‰) and determine whether that scale is resolution-dependent. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/cross-code-simulation-comparison))
- **cross-code-spectra-interpolation-and-residual-analysis**: Use when a new linear perturbation solver's P(k) or CMB spectra must be validated against a benchmark code like CLASS, requiring grid alignment via interpolation and relative/absolute residual quantification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/cross-code-spectra-interpolation-and-residual-analysis))
- **cross-code-yield-comparison**: Use when a newly computed dN/dE photon yield table for dark matter annihilation final states must be validated against established codes, or when quantitatively comparing dark matter codes' indirect-detection outputs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/cross-code-yield-comparison))
- **cross-cohort-component-correlation-analysis**: Use when a component model (e.g., 86 MeCs learned by ICA from integrated TME scRNA-seq) must be shown to be reproducible rather than an artifact of any single training dataset, by testing exclusion of each cohort in turn. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-cohort-component-correlation-analysis))
- **cross-cohort-dataset-scaling**: Use when an scRNA-seq dataset exceeds available RAM (e.g. &gt;0.6M cells on a 128GB machine, or ~11.5M-cell cross-cohort data) and you must run or benchmark annotation pipelines like scaLR, scBalance, scvi, devCellPy, or CellPLM under that limit. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-cohort-dataset-scaling))
- **cross-cohort-expression-consistency-check**: Use when a derived gene read-count or DE table from one RNA-seq cohort must be validated against a second public cohort (e.g. dbGap vs TCGA) before downstream pathway interpretation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-cohort-expression-consistency-check))
- **cross-cohort-gene-expression-correlation**: Use when the same RNA-seq pipeline has been run on two independent patient cohorts and you must quantify whether node-level (FunHoP-aggregated) pathway results — dominant genes and read-count ratios — are consistent across cohorts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-cohort-gene-expression-correlation))
- **cross-cohort-program-network-integration**: Use when two or more expression datasets (bulk/single-cell/spatial, possibly with mismatched features and no shared sample IDs) must be integrated at the program level and annotations such as transcriptional subtypes propagated from a reference cohort to a new cohort. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/cross-cohort-program-network-integration))
- **cross-cohort-proteomics-validation**: Use when recovered/predicted missing proteins from a discovery proteomics run (e.g. Protrec2 output on six matched lung tumor-normal pairs from PXD000603) need independent confirmation in a public cohort such as CPTAC before being reported. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/cross-cohort-proteomics-validation))
- **cross-cohort-risk-score-validation**: Use when a risk score's weights are fitted in a training cohort (e.g. Estonian Biobank, Supplementary Table 4) and must be tested for 5- and 10-year mortality prediction in a separate validation cohort (e.g. FINRISK 1997, 7603 individuals, 1213 deaths). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-cohort-risk-score-validation))
- **cross-cohort-single-cell-integration**: Use when a pan-cancer single-cell integration stage has produced per-cohort ICA z-weight matrices and you must derive cohort-shared transcriptional meta-components instead of dataset-specific components. - **cross-cohort-single-cell-integration**: Use when several scRNA-seq cohorts with responder-status annotations must be jointly scanned for perturbed cell populations, and naive per-cell-type testing would be confounded by individual-to-individual and cohort-to-cohort variability. - **cross-cohort-statistical-reproducibility**: Use when a published finding links a categorical subtype/label (e.g., CMS2 from CMScaller) to a binary drug-response outcome across multiple independent cohorts and the target is to reproduce the reported p-values and odds ratios per cohort. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-cohort-statistical-reproducibility))
- **cross-cohort-subtype-classification**: Use when independent breast cancer RNA-seq cohorts (e.g., GSE96058, PanCA, GSE81538, TCGA) must be assigned PAM50 intrinsic subtypes using a reference computed on a different cohort, and subtype-call concordance must be quantified. - **cross-cohort-tcga-data-pooling-and-harmonization**: Use when you have paired bulk mRNA/miRNA expression matrices spanning several TCGA cancer types and need one harmonized pooled cohort (genes/miRNAs as row names, patients as columns) to compare per-miRNA prediction accuracy across methods. - **cross-cohort-transcriptome-clustering-interpretation**: Use when multiple blood transcriptome cohorts need to be compared at the module-aggregate level using the fixed BloodGen3 repertoire, e.g. to cluster disease states by aggregate activity or reproduce the aggregate abundance heatmap. - **cross-cohort-zscore-normalization**: Use when sgRNA read logFCs from several independent CRISPR screening cohorts (e.g. Freeman, Kearney, Manguso, Pan, Patel, Vredevoogd, Lawson) must be combined into a single consensus gene ranking.
